# Movement review status

This is the current work ledger for all **507 movements**, not a completion certificate. Edit [movement-status.json](movement-status.json), then run `node scripts/generate-movement-status.mjs`. Update affected rows in every progress commit. The family queue selects reusable work; this ledger records each movement’s actual evidence.

Last ledger update: **2026-09-28**. Historical evidence was audited from repository review notes and production code.

- **Assessment:** ✅ Reasonable means the movement looks and works right beside its engraving, with at most invisible modelling limits. 🟡 means small visible differences a careful viewer would notice. ❌ means clear visible or motion defects (wrong shapes, wrong or jerky motion, disconnected or overlapping parts, poor framing). **Visible flaws** lists only what can be seen; **Invisible limits** holds modelling caveats (friction, loads, inferred hidden parts) that do not affect appearance.
- **Visual check:** Yes names the reviewer and means an attributable primary-agent comparison of rendered geometry with the engraving, not original authorship, a test pass, or merely creating a screenshot. Unverified means the record does not establish that attribution; a linked historical review may still exist. A visual check does not mean all flaws were fixed. Changes affecting appearance require a new check.
- **MuJoCo:** live and baked both count as production simulation use. Study only means the installed motion is not MuJoCo-driven. Geometric contact tables and analytically generated animation are not MuJoCo bakes.
- **Self intersections:** includes unintended interpenetration between mechanism parts. Known solver/contact overlap is also disclosed. “None in scoped checks” applies only to the interfaces, phases and tolerances in the cited evidence, not every pair at every instant. Unknown is not clean. Intended joined stock is not itself a defect.

For the next pass, group known intersection defects and concrete motion flaws by reusable component. Then fill visual-review and collision-evidence gaps. Update the affected rows after each correction; do not clear other limitations just because one scoped check passed.

Assessment: reasonable: 505; minor: 2.

Visual: yes: 507.

MuJoCo: none: 435; validation-only: 11; baked: 61.

Intersections: sampled-clear: 459; known: 48.

Risers follow one Archimedean spiral (r = 0.56 + 0.2·θ/2π), and the tread falls 0.26 per turn, so the chain's centre line is a conical helix. There are radial steps only, none around the circumference, and the ledge runs out into the top and the base flange. The chain rides the tread against the riser throughout. The span leaves tangent to the helix, so it rises at the lead angle (2.2–4.2°) where Brown draws it level (forced: a level run would lift the chain off the tread), and the barrel coil follows it. p109: chain end hooked into a block on the base tread; tread normals fixed (no radial streaks). |

The crossed pawls lie just in front of the rack, so the hook webs reaching back to the teeth are short. p91: the pivot pins stop inside the pawl eyes with no front caps; each pawl is one flat plate with nothing in front of it. p96: both crossed pawls are one mirrored construction of lines and arcs: round eyes concentric with the pins, straight arms filleted into them, and hook tips that fill the tooth space (top face flat 0.002 under the tooth, lower face parallel to the flank, square end 0.03 short of the root); no toe past the hook and no notch. Offline MuJoCo bake (3.13.0, docs/p96-u-review.md): lever prescribed; ideal prismatic rack under gravity; pawl torsion springs (preload 0.25 x pawl gravity torque), 3x critical pawl damping, friction 0.15 and common density are assumptions; a 0.0015 hook contact margin keeps loaded hooks about 0.0007 apart; the loop is simulated to closure (2e-7) and starts seated, with no startup settling; the hook webs reaching into the rack plane are reconstructions. p99: three lift swings; the reset takes 4.7 of 32 physical seconds (was 5.4 of 24); loop 16 s displayed, closing to 3.5e-7, starting seated; rebaked. p108: accepted by the user (the demonstration reset is fine). |

Approximate bevel flanks; loaded backlash/friction unvalidated. Pass 92: the crank handle is the shared turned handle on a crank end rounded about its axis. Pass 98: the turned handle runs through the arm as one turned piece, ending 0.005 inside the far face. p101: the driver bearing boss and its bridge are one filleted casting. p109 (user report): the train's topology was verified against the plate and caption (A carries a and h; a–b united with c; h–g united with f; arm k-l on m-n carries d and e; c–d, e–f). The old counts put a outside h, driving h's teeth through b and out through g (the stray fragment). Rebuilt with Brown's arrangement (h outer on A): counts a20 b36 c21 d16 e10 f13 g43 h34 fitted to the plate, frame top arm raised 0.40; the carried train is kept inside a's inner edge (one departure: Brown's head l would sweep into a). Shared smooth bevel builder with fitted tooth phases; no interpenetration over a full carrier turn (193 poses). |
